   27: # File extensions that Database::Abstraction can probe, in probe order.
   28: # .sql, .sqlite, and .sqlite3 are all SQLite databases; .db is Berkeley DB.
   29: Readonly my @SUPPORTED_EXT => qw( csv db sql sqlite sqlite3 xml tsv psv xlsx );
   30: # Use \z (absolute end-of-string) not $ (which permits a trailing \n before \z).
   31: # A query param decoded from "file.csv%0A" has basename "sales.csv\n"; without \z
   32: # that passes the extension guard and reaches realpath with an embedded newline.
   33: Readonly my $EXT_RE        => do { my $p = join '|', @SUPPORTED_EXT; qr/\.(?:$p)\z/i };
   34: 
   35: # Table name safe-identifier pattern -- must match DataSource's TABLE_NAME_RE
   36: # exactly: first character must be a letter or underscore (not a digit), so
   37: # that every table name the controller accepts can also be opened by DataSource
   38: # without an unhandled croak.
   39: Readonly my $TABLE_NAME_RE => qr/\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z/;
   40: 
   41: # URL spec pattern shared by _open_spec and _spec_to_url.
   42: # qr{} delimiter avoids the \/ escaping noise required by the // form.
   43: # /s: makes . match \n so a percent-decoded newline inside a URL does not
   44: # silently truncate the captured URL before the \z end-of-string anchor.
   45: Readonly my $URL_SPEC_RE   => qr{\Aurl:(https?://.+)\z}si;
   46: 
   47: # Redis spec pattern: redis:tablename|redis://[password@]host[:port][/db_index]
   48: # The pipe separates the table name from the connection URL so the URL's own
   49: # colons and slashes do not require extra quoting.
   50: Readonly my $REDIS_SPEC_RE => qr{\Aredis:([^|]+)\|(redis://.+)\z}si;

  157: # _is_safe_url($url) -> bool
  158: #
  159: # Purpose: SSRF guard.  Blocks the most dangerous classes of Server-Side Request
  160: #          Forgery targets: loopback aliases (localhost, 127/8) and literal
  161: #          private/link-local/CGNAT IPv4 addresses in the URL host component.
  162: #
  163: # Design rationale for scope:
  164: #   Resolving hostnames via DNS and checking the result is ineffective: a
  165: #   separate DNS lookup happens at LWP connect time (TOCTOU / DNS rebinding
  166: #   window).  The authoritative defence against hostname-based SSRF is a
  167: #   network-layer egress firewall.  This function handles the Perl-layer
  168: #   interception for the most common patterns that operators cannot easily
  169: #   filter at the network level: bare loopback aliases and literal private IPs
  170: #   hard-coded by an attacker.
  171: #
  172: # Blocked targets:
  173: #   localhost / 127.0.0.0/8 / 0.0.0.0 / ::1  -- loopback aliases
  174: #   10.0.0.0/8    -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  175: #   172.16.0.0/12 -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  176: #   192.168.0.0/16-- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  177: #   169.254.0.0/16-- link-local; AWS/GCP/Azure metadata endpoint (literal IP)
  178: #   100.64.0.0/10 -- CGNAT / Tailscale shared space (literal IP)
  179: #
  180: # Hostname-based targets (e.g. http://internal.corp.example.com/) are allowed

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  181: # at this layer; block them with egress firewall rules instead.
  182: sub _is_safe_url {
  183: 	my $url = $_[0];
  184: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  185: 	return 0 unless $url =~ m{\Ahttps?://([^/:?\[\]#]+)}i;
  186: 	my $host = lc $1;
  187: 
  188: 	# Block well-known loopback aliases.
  189: 	return 0 if $host eq 'localhost'
  190: 	          || $host =~ /\A127\./
  191: 	          || $host eq '0.0.0.0'
  192: 	          || $host eq '::1';

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  193: 
  194: 	# For literal IPv4 addresses only: check private/link-local/CGNAT ranges.
  195: 	# Skipping DNS for hostname targets avoids a live network call in tests and
  196: 	# removes the TOCTOU window that makes DNS-resolved checks illusory anyway.
  197: 	return 1 unless $host =~ /\A\d{1,3}(?:\.\d{1,3}){3}\z/;

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)

  210: # _safe_back_url($url) -> $url | undef
  211: #
  212: # Purpose: Sanitise a user-supplied back-link URL before it is passed to the
  213: #          template and rendered inside an href="" attribute.  Template Toolkit's
  214: #          | html filter encodes <>&" but does NOT block the javascript: or
  215: #          data: URI schemes, so a raw param('back') = "javascript:alert(1)"
  216: #          would be rendered verbatim inside the href and execute on click.
  217: #
  218: # Rule:    Only absolute http(s) URLs and root-relative paths (starting with /)
  219: #          are permitted.  Anything else (javascript:, data:, vbscript:, empty

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  220: #          strings, undef) returns undef so the template renders no href.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  221: #

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  222: sub _safe_back_url {
  223: 	my ($url) = @_;
  224: 	return undef unless defined $url && length($url);
  225: 	return $url if $url =~ m{\A(?:/(?!/)|https?://)}i;
  226: 	return undef;
  227: }

  451: # _apply_filter_spec($records, $spec) -> \@filtered_records
  452: #
  453: # Purpose: Apply one "col:op:val" filter to an arrayref of record hashrefs and
  454: #          return a new (possibly shorter) arrayref.  The original is not mutated.
  455: #          Value may contain colons: "col:op:val:with:colons" -- split limit 3
  456: #          puts everything after the second colon into $val.
  457: #          Unknown operators fall through to 1 (all rows pass) so new operators
  458: #          added in future do not break existing callers.
  459: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $spec is a colon-delimited string.
  460: # Exit:    Returns filtered arrayref (same reference if spec is invalid/unparseable).
  461: #
  462: # Domain Constraints (operator names):
  463: #   Operator names are CASE-SENSITIVE.  Only the exact lowercase forms below are
  464: #   recognised; an uppercase or mixed-case name (e.g. "EQ", "Lt") falls through
  465: #   to the default `1` branch and all rows pass unfiltered.
  466: #   Valid operators:
  467: #     String (value comparison is case-insensitive via lc()):
  468: #       eq, ne, contains, starts
  469: #     Numeric (Perl <, <=, >, >= coercion):
  470: #       lt (strict), le (inclusive), gt (strict), ge (inclusive)
  471: #     Unary (val is ignored):
  472: #       empty (cell eq ''), notempty (cell ne '')
  473: #   Boundary rules for numeric operators:
  474: #     le/ge include the boundary value; lt/gt exclude it.
  475: #   contains/starts with empty val ('') match every record (index always 0).
  476: #
  477: # Reduction: split(/:/, $spec, 3) always produces at least one defined element,
  478: # so "defined $col" is structurally guaranteed true and is removed.
  479: # "$_->{$col} // ''" replaces the equivalent ternary form.
  480: #
  481: # Optimisation: $lval = lc($val) is computed once before the grep.  Without this
  482: # precompute, every case-insensitive operator (eq, ne, contains, starts) would
  483: # call lc($val) O(N) times for N records -- a pure O(N) waste since $val is

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  484: # constant within a single filter application.
  485: sub _apply_filter_spec {
  486: 	my ($records, $spec) = @_;
  487: 	my ($col, $op, $val) = split /:/, $spec, 3;
  488: 	return $records unless length($col // '') && defined $op && length $op;
  489: 	$val //= '';
  490: 	my $lval = lc $val;	# precompute once; avoids O(N) redundant lc() inside grep

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  491: 	return [grep {

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  492: 		my $cell = $_->{$col} // '';

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  493: 		$op eq 'eq'       ? lc($cell) eq $lval                :

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  494: 		$op eq 'ne'       ? lc($cell) ne $lval                :

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  495: 		$op eq 'contains' ? index(lc($cell), $lval) != -1     :

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  496: 		$op eq 'starts'   ? index(lc($cell), $lval) == 0      :
  497: 		$op eq 'lt'       ? $cell <  $val                     :
  498: 		$op eq 'le'       ? $cell <= $val                     :
  499: 		$op eq 'gt'       ? $cell >  $val                     :
  500: 		$op eq 'ge'       ? $cell >= $val                     :
  501: 		$op eq 'empty'    ? $cell eq ''                        :
  502: 		$op eq 'notempty' ? $cell ne ''                        :
  503: 		1
  504: 	} @$records];
  505: }

  507: # _apply_filters($self, $records) -> ($filtered_records, \@raw_specs, $json_string)

  512: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs.

  517: # apply filters, one to parse them for JSON.  By Modus Ponens, _apply_filter_spec
  518: # returns $records unchanged for a malformed spec (same guard condition), so
  519: # skipping the call for malformed specs is equivalent.  Merging into one loop

  552: # _filter_specs_to_criteria( \@specs, $source ) -> ( \%criteria, \@residual_specs )
  553: #
  554: # Purpose: Map the f= filter vocabulary onto Database::Abstraction / Database::Join
  555: #          criteria hashrefs so the backend can apply pushable filters before
  556: #          paginating.  Specs that cannot be safely pushed are returned as
  557: #          @residual_specs for subsequent in-memory application.
  558: #
  559: # Pushdown is only performed when $source->dbi_source() confirms a SQL (SQLite)
  560: # backend.  For CSV (slurp path), in-memory, XLSX, BerkeleyDB and URL sources
  561: # everything is placed in @residual_specs so the existing _apply_filter_spec
  562: # Perl path handles them.
  563: #
  564: # Pushable mapping (SQLite only):
  565: #   eq / ne         -- LIKE / NOT LIKE without wildcards.  SQLite LIKE is
  566: #                      case-insensitive for ASCII, matching the existing Perl
  567: #                      lc() semantics.  Values containing % or _ are pushed to
  568: #                      residual to avoid LIKE metacharacter conflicts.
  569: #   contains        -- LIKE '%val%'.  Residual when value contains % or _.
  570: #   starts          -- LIKE 'val%'.   Residual when value contains % or _.
  571: #   lt / le / gt / ge -- SQL numeric comparison.  Residual when the value is
  572: #                      not a plain number (avoids column-affinity surprises
  573: #                      with currency or comma-formatted values).
  574: #   empty / notempty -- Always residual.  Must match both SQL NULL and the
  575: #                      literal empty string; cannot be expressed as a single
  576: #                      D::A criterion without an -or grouping.

  578: # Column collision: if a column already has a pushable criterion, the second
  579: # spec for that column is placed in @residual_specs.  This keeps the AND logic

  665: # _numeric_cell($v) -> $number | undef
  666: #
  667: # Extract a numeric value from a raw cell, handling currency prefixes
  668: # (e.g. $1,234.56) and accounting-notation negatives ((1,234.56) -> -1234.56).
  669: # Returns undef for empty, undef, or non-numeric input.
  670: # Used by the Perl fold path of _grouped_totals so all four chart routes
  671: # share one extraction function.
  672: sub _numeric_cell {
  673: 	my $v = shift;
  674: 	return undef unless defined $v && length($v);
  675: 	my $is_acct_neg = ($v =~ /\A\s*\(/);
  676: 	(my $n = $v) =~ s/[^\d.\-]//g;
  677: 	$n = "-$n" if $is_acct_neg && $n =~ /\A\d/;
  678: 	return undef unless $n =~ /\A-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?\z/;
  679: 	return $n + 0;
  680: }

  877: # _fetch_page( $self, $source, \%params ) -> ( $records, $has_next )
  878: #   params: per_page  -- page size (required)
  879: #           offset    -- 0-based row offset (required)
  880: #           dedup     -- truthy when ?d=1 dedup is requested
  881: #           sort_col  -- column name to sort by (optional)
  882: #           sort_dir  -- 'asc' or 'desc' (default 'asc'; ignored unless sort_col set)
  883: #
  884: # Purpose: Fetch and reduce one page of records from $source, honouring the
  885: #          reduce-before-paginate ordering contract.  Two modes:
  886: #
  887: #   Fast path (no f= filters, no dedup): uses the N+1 trick
  888: #     selectall_arrayref(limit => per_page+1, offset => offset).  has_next is
  889: #     determined by the extra row before it is popped.
  890: #
  891: #   Reduction path (f= filters or dedup present): materialises the full result
  892: #     in memory, applies all f= filters with _apply_filter_spec (case-insensitive),
  893: #     applies dedup if requested, then paginates in Perl.  This guarantees that
  894: #     the Nth page contains the Nth page of the *reduced* set, not the Nth page
  895: #     of the raw set reduced to fewer rows.  Memory cost: the full reduced set.
  896: #
  897: # Sort: sort_col/sort_dir are forwarded to selectall_arrayref as
  898: #   sort_by => [$sort_col, $sort_dir].  Both Database::Abstraction 0.49+
  899: #   and Database::Join 0.009.0 honour this parameter natively.

  901: # Returns (undef, 0) when the underlying fetch throws; the caller must handle
  902: # $records being undef.

 1455: # _serialize_csv($records, \@columns) -> $utf8_string
 1456: #
 1457: # Purpose: Build a complete RFC 4180 CSV document (header + data rows) from

 1464: # The previous pattern ($out .= _csv_row(...) per row) triggers O(N) string

 1466: # For exports of thousands of rows the difference is measurable.
 1467: #
 1468: # XS note: for very large exports, replacing _csv_row with Text::CSV_XS
 1469: # (available from CPAN) would further reduce serialisation time -- its C-level
 1470: # implementation is roughly 5-10x faster than the pure-Perl version here.
 1471: sub _serialize_csv {
●1472 → 1474 → 1477 1472: 	my ($records, $columns) = @_;

 1474: 	for my $row (@$records) {
 1475: 		push @lines, _csv_row(map { $row->{$_} } @$columns);
 1476: 	}
 1477: 	return encode('UTF-8', join('', @lines));
 1478: }

 1527:

 1529: 
 1530: None (reads C<data_dir> from application config).
 1531: 
 1532: =head4 OUTPUT
 1533: 
 1534: Renders C<home.html.tt> with:
 1535: 
 1536:   tables     ARRAYREF of { name => $stem, file => $basename }
 1537:   title      'Choose a Database'

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1538: 
 1539: =head3 MESSAGES
 1540: 
 1541: None produced by this action; any file-scan errors are silently ignored
 1542: (empty directory yields an empty card grid).
 1543: 
 1544: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 1545: 
 1546:   index == lambda self .
 1547:     let dir  = resolve self.app.config.data_dir in
 1548:     let tbls = { b | b in dir /\ basename(b) =~ EXT_RE } in
 1549:     post render(home, tables: map(stem, tbls))
 1550: 
 1551: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1552: 
 1553:   GET / -> 200 text/html containing a card for each file in data/
 1554: 
 1555: =cut
 1556: 
 1557: sub index ($self) {
 1558: 	my ($platform, $language) = $self->_resolve_template;
 1559: 
 1560: 	$self->render(
 1561: 		template => "$platform/$language/home",
 1562: 		handler  => 'tt',
 1563: 		format   => 'html',
 1564: 		tables   => $self->_scan_data_dir,
 1565: 		title    => 'Choose a Database',
 1566: 	);
 1567: }
 1568: 
 1569: =head2 view
 1570: 
 1571: C<GET /view/:table> -- Open and display the chosen table from C<data_dir>.
 1572: 
 1573: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1574: 
 1575: =head4 INPUT
 1576: 
 1577:   :table   string   Table name (alphanumeric + underscore).  Returns 404 on
 1578:                     other characters.
 1579:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 1580: 
 1581: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: :table
 1582: 
 1583: C<:table> is validated against C<\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z> before C<open_table>
 1584: is ever called.  The first character must be a letter (A-Z, a-z) or an
 1585: underscore; subsequent characters may also include digits (0-9).
 1586: 
 1587: =over 4
 1588: 
 1589: =item Valid partition
 1590: 
 1591: C<sales> (letters only), C<_temp> (underscore-start), C<report_2024>
 1592: (mixed).  A name that is valid but has no backing file returns 200 with
 1593: an error message -- NOT 404.
 1594: 
 1595: =item Invalid partition
 1596: 
 1597: C<1sales> (digit-start, 404), C<my.data> (dot, 404), C<my-data>
 1598: (hyphen, 404), non-ASCII characters (404).
 1599: 
 1600: =item Boundary values
 1601:

 1812: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: ?path=
 1813: 
 1814: =over 4
 1815: 
 1816: =item Extension filter (C<EXT_RE>)
 1817: 
 1818: The basename must match C<\.(?:csv|db|sql|xml|tsv|psv)\z> (case-insensitive).

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 1819: The C<\z> anchor (absolute end-of-string) means a URL-encoded trailing
 1820: newline (e.g. C<file.csv%0A> decoded to C<file.csv\n>) does NOT pass --
 1821: the C<\n> falls after the C<\z> boundary and the extension check fails.
 1822: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 1823: =item Valid partition
 1824: 
 1825: C</data/sales.csv> (lowercase extension), C</tmp/REPORT.CSV> (uppercase
 1826: extension, /i matches), any C<.db>, C<.sql>, C<.xml>, C<.tsv>, C<.psv> regular
 1827: file.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1828: 
 1829: =item Invalid partition
 1830: 
 1831: Absent C<?path=> (404), non-existent file (404), directory instead of
 1832: file (404), unsupported extension such as C<.txt> (404), empty string
 1833: (404), path containing a C<%0A> (newline) suffix (404).
 1834: 
 1835: =item Double-extension filenames
 1836: 
 1837: A file like C<file.php.csv> passes C<EXT_RE> (last segment is C<.csv>)
 1838: but produces table stem C<file.php> which fails C<DataSource>'s
 1839: C<TABLE_NAME_RE>.  The C<open_table> call is inside C<eval>, so the
 1840: croak is caught and the action returns 200 with a friendly error, not
 1841: a 500.
 1842: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 1843: =back

 2460: =head2 combine_tables

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2461: 
 2462: C<GET /combine> -- Stack rows from two or more tables vertically into a single
 2463: unified view.
 2464: 
 2465: Unlike C</join> (which appends columns from a matching row in a second table),
 2466: C</combine> appends the I<rows> from a second table beneath the rows of the

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 2467: first.  All columns from all sources appear as headers; where a row has no
 2468: value for a particular column (because that column did not exist in its source
 2469: file) the cell is left blank.
 2470: 
 2471: This is equivalent to a SQL C<UNION ALL> across heterogeneous schemas.
 2472: 
 2473: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2474: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2475: =head4 INPUT
 2476: 
 2477:   ?l=    string   (required) Left table spec: "table:name" or "path:/abs/path".
 2478:                   Returns 404 if unresolvable.
 2479:   ?c=    string   (repeatable) Additional table spec to stack beneath the
 2480:                   current result.  Invalid or non-existent specs are silently
 2481:                   skipped.
 2482:   ?f=    string   (repeatable) Result filter: "col:op:val".
 2483: 
 2484: =head4 OUTPUT
 2485: 
 2486: Renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the combined result set.
 2487: 
 2488: =head3 MESSAGES
 2489: 
 2490:   error_table_open   -- Left table fetch threw (re-renders home with error).
 2491: 
 2492: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 2493: 
 2494:   combine_tables == lambda self .
 2495:     let left = open_spec(param('l')) in
 2496:     pre  left /= undef
 2497:     let sources  = [left] ++ map(open_spec, param_list('c')) in
 2498:     let combined = _combine_tables(sources) in
 2499:     let filtered = fold(_apply_filter_spec, combined, param_list('f')) in
 2500:     post render(dashboard, records: filtered)
 2501: 
 2502: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2503: 
 2504:   GET /combine?l=table:cats&c=table:dogs
 2505:     -> unified view: all cat and dog rows; Species/Name/Color/Breed/Eye Color
 2506:        appear for both; Environment (cats-only) and Sex/Fixed (dogs-only)
 2507:        are blank where the source file lacks that column.
 2508: 
 2509: =cut

 2968: 
 2969: C<POST /upload> -- Accept a drag-and-dropped data file and return a redirect URL.
 2970: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 2971: The file is saved under its original filename in a managed subdirectory of
 2972: C<< <app_home>/.uploads/ >>.  The subdirectory name is randomised so that
 2973: concurrent uploads of files with the same name do not collide.
 2974: 
 2975: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2976: 
 2977: =head4 INPUT

 2979: Multipart form upload, field name: C<file>.
 2980: 
 2981:   file   upload   Supported extensions: csv, db, sql, xml, tsv, psv.

 2983: =head4 OUTPUT
 2984: 
 2985:   200 application/json   { "url": "/open?path=/abs/path/file.csv", "path": "/abs/path/file.csv" }
 2986:   400 application/json   { "error": "No file received" }
 2987:   413 application/json   { "error": "File too large to upload (limit: 50 MB)" }
 2988:   415 application/json   { "error": "Unsupported file type. Accepted: ..." }
 2989:   500 application/json   { "error": "Could not save uploaded file" }

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 2990: 
 2991: =head3 MESSAGES
 2992: 
 2993:   error_upload_none       -- no file was received in the multipart upload.
 2994:   error_upload_ext        -- the file's extension is not in the supported list.
 2995:   error_upload_too_large  -- the upload body exceeded MAX_UPLOAD_MIB (50 MiB).
 2996:   error_upload_save       -- move_to failed or the destination file was not
 2997:                              created (I/O error, full disk, permission error).
 2998: 
 2999: =head3 IMPLEMENTATION NOTES
 3000: 
 3001: The C<move_to> call is wrapped with the C<eval { ...; 1 }> idiom (not
 3002: C<eval {}> + C<if ($@)>).  Mojolicious dispatch and DESTROY-phase evals can
 3003: leave C<$@> set before the action runs; checking C<if ($@)> after a new eval
 3004: may fire falsely on that stale value.  C<eval { move_to(); 1 }> returns 1 on
 3005: success and C<undef> on exception, so the result is independent of any prior
 3006: C<$@> value.
 3007: 
 3008: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3009: 
 3010:   upload_file == lambda self .

 3012:     pre  upload /= undef /\ basename(upload.filename) =~ EXT_RE
 3013:          /\ NOT req->is_limit_exceeded

 3017:     pre  ok /\ -f dest

 3019: 
 3020: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3021: 
 3022:   POST /upload  (multipart: file=@sales.csv)
 3023:     -> { "url": "/open?path=%2F...%2Fsales.csv", "path": "/.../.uploads/abc123/sales.csv" }

 3403: 
 3404: =head2 heatmap_view
 3405: 
 3406: C<GET /heatmap> -- Render a D3.js grid heatmap for two categorical axes.
 3407: 
 3408: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 3409: 

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
 3410:   x=<col>      X-axis (horizontal) categorical column (required)
 3411:   y=<col>      Y-axis (vertical) categorical column (required)
 3412:   val=<col>    Cell value column (optional; when absent, cells show row count)
 3413:   scheme=<name> D3 sequential colour scheme name (default: YlOrRd)
 3414:   show_val=1   Overlay the numeric value inside each cell
 3415:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 3416: 
 3417: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3418: 
 3419: =head4 INPUT
 3420: 
 3421:   l       spec     Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 3422:   x       string   Required.  Name of the X-axis categorical column.
 3423:   y       string   Required.  Name of the Y-axis categorical column.
 3424:   val     string   Optional.  Name of the value column; omit for count mode.
 3425:   scheme  string   Optional.  D3 sequential colour scheme name.
 3426:   show_val flag    Optional.  Set to 1 to render values inside cells.
 3427:   back    url      Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 3428: 
 3429: =head4 OUTPUT
 3430:

 4710: # dates_save: POST /dates/save?l=<spec>&col=<col>&pattern=<fmt>
 4711: # Stores a strptime-style date normalisation pattern for $col in models.json.
 4712: # Only the three patterns in %ALLOWED_DATE_PATTERNS are accepted.
 4713: sub dates_save ($self) {
 4714: 	my $spec    = $self->param('l')       // '';
 4715: 	my $col     = $self->param('col')     // '';

 4717: 
 4718: 	return $self->render(json => { error => 'l= required' }, status => 400)
 4719: 		unless length $spec;

 4721: 		unless length $col;
 4722: 	return $self->render(
 4723: 		json   => { error => $self->_i18n('error_date_pattern', $pattern) },
 4724: 		status => 400,
 4725: 	) unless $ALLOWED_DATE_PATTERNS{$pattern};
 4726: 
 4727: 	my $models = $self->_read_models;
 4728: 	$models->{date_patterns}{$spec}{$col} = $pattern;
 4729: 	$self->_write_models($models);
 4730: 	$self->render(json => { ok => 1 });
 4731: }

 5088: 	my ($source_url, $source_accessed) = $self->_url_attribution;
 5089: 
 5090: 	require HTML::D3;
 5091: 	my $title   = $count_mode ? "Treemap of $cat_col by count" : "Treemap of $cat_col by $val_col";

 5093: 		->render_treemap_snippet(\@data, {
 5094: 			scheme => $scheme,
 5095: 		});
 5096:

 5098: 	utf8::decode($treemap_html) unless utf8::is_utf8($treemap_html);
 5099:

 5101: 	$self->render(
 5102: 		handler         => 'tt',

 5105: 		title           => $title,

 5108: 		cat_col         => $cat_col,
 5109: 		drill_back_url  => $back,
 5110: 		back_url        => ($back2 // $back),
 5111: 		back_label      => ($back2 ? ($back2_lbl || 'Back') : 'Back to table'),
 5112: 		($back2 ? (back2_url => $back, back2_label => 'Back to table') : ()),
 5113: 		source_url      => $source_url,
 5114: 		source_accessed => $source_accessed,
 5115: 	);
 5116: }

 5193: =head1 NAME
 5194: 
 5195: Database::BI::Controller::Dashboard - Home picker, filesystem browser, table
 5196: viewer, left-join engine, result filter, export, and file upload
 5197: 
 5198: =head1 SYNOPSIS
 5199: 
 5200: All routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.  You do not
 5201: call its methods directly -- Mojolicious dispatches HTTP requests to them
 5202: automatically.  The examples below show browser URLs and their curl
 5203: equivalents.
 5204: 
 5205: B<Open the home page and see all tables in the data directory:>
 5206: 
 5207:   # Browser
 5208:   http://localhost:3000/
 5209: 
 5210:   # curl
 5211:   curl http://localhost:3000/
 5212: 
 5213: B<View a single table (file: data/sales.csv):>
 5214: 
 5215:   # Browser
 5216:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 5217: 
 5218:   # curl
 5219:   curl http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 5220: 
 5221: B<Filter rows -- show only rows where region equals "North":>
 5222: 
 5223:   # Browser (add ?f=col:op:val to any view URL)
 5224:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North
 5225: 
 5226:   # Multiple filters -- "North" AND amount greater than 100:
 5227:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North&f=amount:gt:100
 5228: 
 5229:   # curl
 5230:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North'
 5231: 
 5232: B<Join two tables -- left-join sales with products on the product column:>
 5233: 
 5234:   # Browser
 5235:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 5236: 
 5237:   # The j= parameter is: right-table-spec | left-key | right-key
 5238:   # You can chain multiple joins:
 5239:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&j=table:regions|region|id
 5240: 
 5241: B<Open a file anywhere on the filesystem (not just in data/):>
 5242: 
 5243:   http://localhost:3000/open?path=/home/user/reports/q3.csv
 5244: 
 5245: B<Download the current view as a CSV file:>
 5246: 
 5247:   # Uses the same l=, j=, f= parameters as /join
 5248:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=csv
 5249: 
 5250:   # Download as a SQLite database file instead:
 5251:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=sqlite
 5252: 
 5253:   # Download a filtered + joined result:
 5254:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&f=region:eq:North&format=csv
 5255: 
 5256: B<Save the current view to a file on the server (instead of downloading):>
 5257: 
 5258:   # POST with form fields; format is inferred from the filename extension
 5259:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 5260:        -F l=table:sales \
 5261:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 5262:        -F filename=report.csv
 5263: 
 5264:   # Save as SQLite:
 5265:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 5266:        -F l=table:sales \
 5267:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 5268:        -F filename=report.sql
 5269: 
 5270: B<Get the column list for a table (used by the join UI):>
 5271: 
 5272:   curl http://localhost:3000/api/columns?table=sales
 5273:   # Returns: {"columns":["product","region","amount","date"]}
 5274: 
 5275: B<Check when a file was last modified (used by the tooltip on the home page):>
 5276: 
 5277:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/api/stat?path=/data/sales.csv'
 5278:   # Returns: {"exists":true,"path":"/data/sales.csv","mtime":1700000000,"size":1234}
 5279: 
 5280: B<Browse the filesystem to find a data file:>
 5281: 
 5282:   http://localhost:3000/browse
 5283:   http://localhost:3000/browse?path=/home/user/data
 5284: 
 5285: B<Upload a data file by dropping it onto the page (multipart form POST):>
 5286: 
 5287:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/upload \
 5288:        -F file=@/home/user/data/sales.csv
 5289:   # Returns: {"url":"/open?path=/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv","path":"/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv"}
 5290: 
 5291: =head2 graph_view
 5292: 
 5293: C<GET /graph> -- Render a D3.js v7 line chart from the current data pipeline.
 5294: 
 5295: Accepts the same pipeline parameters as L</join_tables> (C<l=>, C<j=>,
 5296: C<c=>, C<f=>, C<d=>) plus:
 5297: 
 5298: =over 4
 5299: 
 5300: =item C<x=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 5301: 
 5302: Column name to use as the X axis (required).  Any data type is accepted;
 5303: values are displayed as categorical labels.
 5304: 
 5305: =item C<y=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 5306: 
 5307: Column name to use as the Y axis (required).  Non-numeric characters
 5308: (currency symbols, commas, etc.) are stripped before comparison; rows
 5309: where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are silently skipped.
 5310: 
 5311: =item C<back=E<lt>urlE<gt>>
 5312: 
 5313: URL for the "Back to table" link rendered in the graph toolbar.  Defaults
 5314: to C</> when absent.  The dashboard JS sets this to the current page URL
 5315: before navigating.
 5316: 
 5317: =back
 5318: 
 5319: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 5320: 
 5321: =head4 INPUT
 5322: 
 5323:   l=<spec>     string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 5324:   x=<col>      string   X-axis column name (required).
 5325:   y=<col>      string   Y-axis column name (required; must be numeric).
 5326:   back=<url>   string   Back-link URL (optional; default "/").
 5327:   j=, c=, f=, d=        Pipeline params -- see L</join_tables>.
 5328: 
 5329: =head4 OUTPUT
 5330: 
 5331:   200 text/html   TT-rendered graph page.  The HTML::D3 snippet embeds a
 5332:                   C<const data = [...]> JSON array where each element has:
 5333:                     { label: <x-value>, value: <y-value>,
 5334:                       extra: { <col>: <val>, ... } }
 5335:                   C<extra> contains every column not used as X or Y, so
 5336:                   the hover tooltip shows the complete row for each point.
 5337:                   The chart supports brush-to-zoom; a "Reset zoom" button
 5338:                   appears after a brush selection.  The toolbar shows the
 5339:                   count of plotted data points.
 5340: 
 5341:   400 text/plain  C<x=> or C<y=> absent, or named column not found.
 5342:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 5343: 
 5344: =head3 MESSAGES
 5345: 
 5346:   Missing x or y column parameter          C<x=> or C<y=> query param absent.
 5347:   Column not found: <name>                  Named column absent from result set.
 5348:   Could not open data source                Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 5349:   No plottable data: no rows have valid ... All rows lack a numeric Y value.
 5350: 
 5351: =head3 EXAMPLE
 5352: 
 5353:   GET /graph?l=table:sales&x=product&y=amount
 5354: 
 5355: Renders a line chart of C<amount> (Y) against C<product> (X).  Mousing over
 5356: a dot shows a tooltip with the product name, the amount, and every other
 5357: column in that row (e.g. C<region>, C<date>).
 5358: 
 5359: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5360: 
 5361:   graph_view : Controller Ã— Params â†’ HTML | Error
 5362: 
 5363:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, c, f, d)
 5364:   let pairs    = [ [row[x], num(row[y]), row \ {x,y}]
 5365:                    | row âˆˆ pipeline.records,  num(row[y]) â‰  âŠ¥ ]
 5366: 
 5367:   pre  x âˆˆ params âˆ§ y âˆˆ params           else 400
 5368:   pre  x âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 5369:   pre  y âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 5370:   pre  pairs â‰  []                          else 200 "No plottable data"
 5371:   post HTML::D3->render_line_chart_snippet(pairs) embedded in TT layout
 5372: 
 5373: =cut
 5374: 
 5375: =head2 pie_view
 5376: 
 5377: C<GET /pie> -- Render a D3.js v7 animated pie chart from the current data
 5378: pipeline.
 5379: 
 5380: Each row's category value (C<cat=>) is used as a slice label; the numeric
 5381: value column (C<val=>) is summed per category.  Accounting-notation
 5382: negatives such as C<(1,234.56)> are handled automatically.  Slices are
 5383: sorted by total descending; when there are more than 12 distinct categories
 5384: the smallest ones are collapsed into a single "Other" slice.
 5385: 
 5386: Clicking any slice or legend entry navigates back to the table view with
 5387: an C<f=cat:eq:value> filter applied automatically, so the user can drill
 5388: into the data behind a slice without leaving the application.
 5389: 
 5390: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 5391: 
 5392: =head4 INPUT
 5393: 
 5394:   l=<spec>    string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 5395:   cat=<col>   string   Category column name (required; used as slice labels).
 5396:   val=<col>   string   Numeric value column name (required; values are summed).
 5397:   donut=1     flag     Render as a donut chart with a hole in the centre (optional).
 5398:   back=<url>  string   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional; default "/").
 5399:   f=<spec>    string   Result filter (repeatable; same syntax as /join).
 5400: 
 5401: =head4 OUTPUT
 5402: 
 5403:   200 text/html   TT-rendered pie chart page containing:
 5404:                   - Two-level breadcrumb (Home > Back to table).
 5405:                   - Export buttons for SVG and PNG (legend is composited in).
 5406:                   - An animated D3.js pie or donut chart with a colour-coded
 5407:                     legend.  Each slice and legend entry is clickable and
 5408:                     navigates to the filtered table view.
 5409:                   - Slice count (e.g. "7 slices").
 5410: 
 5411:   200 text/plain  "No plottable data" when every row has a non-numeric or
 5412:                   missing value column (HTTP 200, not 4xx, so the page can
 5413:                   render a friendly message).
 5414: 
 5415:   400 text/plain  C<cat=> or C<val=> absent, or named column not found.
 5416:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 5417: 
 5418: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS
 5419: 
 5420:   cat=    Any column name that exists in the result set.  Cell values are
 5421:           used verbatim as slice labels.  Empty-string cells are skipped.
 5422: 
 5423:   val=    Must be a column whose cells contain numbers (after stripping
 5424:           currency symbols, commas, and handling accounting-notation negatives).
 5425:           Rows where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are skipped.
 5426:           Negative totals are included in the chart (slice values may be negative
 5427:           if the source data contains credits or reversals).
 5428: 
 5429:   donut=  Any truthy value (e.g. "1") enables the donut hole.  Absent or
 5430:           "0" renders a solid pie.
 5431: 
 5432: =head3 MESSAGES
 5433: 
 5434:   Missing cat or val column parameter    C<cat=> or C<val=> query param absent.
 5435:   Column not found: <name>               Named column absent from the result set.
 5436:   Could not open data source             Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 5437:   No plottable data: ...                 All rows lack a valid numeric value.
 5438: 
 5439: =head3 EXAMPLE
 5440: 
 5441:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&back=/view/sales
 5442: 
 5443: Renders a pie chart of total C<amount> per C<region>.  Clicking the
 5444: "North" slice navigates to C</view/sales?f=region:eq:North>.
 5445: 
 5446: With a donut hole:
 5447: 
 5448:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&donut=1
 5449: 
 5450: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5451: 
 5452:   pie_view : Controller x Params -> HTML | Error
 5453: 
 5454:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, f, d)
 5455:   let totals   = { cat => sum { val(row) | row in pipeline.records,
 5456:                                            cat(row) != '', num(val(row)) != bot }
 5457:                  | cat in range(cat_col) }
 5458: 
 5459:   pre  cat in params /\ val in params     else 400
 5460:   pre  cat in pipeline.cols               else 400
 5461:   pre  val in pipeline.cols               else 400
 5462:   pre  totals != {}                        else 200 "No plottable data"
 5463:   post HTML::D3->render_pie_chart_snippet(slices) embedded in TT layout
 5464:        where slices = sort_desc { |s.value| | s in totals }
 5465: 
 5466: =cut
 5467: 
 5468: =head2 clear_uploads
 5469: 
 5470: C<POST /uploads/clear> -- Delete every file from the C<.uploads/> staging
 5471: directory and return the total disk space recovered.
 5472: 
 5473: The C<.uploads/> directory accumulates files from drag-and-drop uploads.
 5474: It grows indefinitely; this action lets users reclaim that space without
 5475: needing shell access.
 5476: 
 5477: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 5478: 
 5479: =head4 INPUT
 5480: 
 5481: No parameters.  The C<.uploads/> path is always the one under C<app->home>.
 5482: 
 5483: =head4 OUTPUT
 5484: 
 5485:   200 application/json   { "freed": <bytes>, "count": <n> }
 5486: 
 5487: C<freed> is the total number of bytes removed.  C<count> is the number of
 5488: files deleted.  Both are 0 when the directory is absent or already empty.
 5489: 
 5490: =cut
 5491: 
 5492: =head1 DESCRIPTION
 5493: 
 5494: All user-facing routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.
 5495: See the individual action POD above for per-endpoint documentation.
 5496: 
 5497: =head2 Filter operators and the quick-filter bar
 5498: 
 5499: Filters are expressed as C<f=col:op:val> query parameters and are applied
 5500: server-side after all joins.  Multiple C<f=> params are applied in order
 5501: (AND semantics).
 5502: 
 5503: The colon separator is split with a limit of 3, so values may themselves
 5504: contain colons (e.g. C<f=sale_date:eq:2025-01-15>, or a time value like
 5505: C<f=start_time:eq:14:30:00>).
 5506: 
 5507: B<Supported operators:>
 5508: 
 5509:   eq        case-insensitive string equality
 5510:   ne        case-insensitive string inequality
 5511:   contains  case-insensitive substring match
 5512:   starts    case-insensitive prefix match
 5513:   lt        numeric less-than
 5514:   le        numeric less-than-or-equal
 5515:   gt        numeric greater-than
 5516:   ge        numeric greater-than-or-equal
 5517:   empty     cell is undef or empty string (val ignored)
 5518:   notempty  cell is defined and non-empty (val ignored)
 5519: 
 5520: B<Quick-filter bar (table view):>
 5521: 
 5522: A filter row below the toolbar lets the user pick a column, operator, and
 5523: value and press "Apply filter" (or Enter) to add a condition without
 5524: opening any panel.  The current URL is updated with the new C<f=> param
 5525: and the page reloads; all other URL parameters (joins, dedup, etc.) are
 5526: preserved automatically via the browser C<URL> API.
 5527: 
 5528: Active filter chips appear in the toolbar.  Each chip has an individual
 5529: x-button to remove just that condition.  The "x Clear filters" link
 5530: removes all C<f=> params at once.
 5531: 
 5532: B<Drill-down from pie chart:>
 5533: 
 5534: Clicking a pie slice or legend entry on C</pie> navigates to the table
 5535: view with C<f=cat_col:eq:label> appended, so the user can inspect the
 5536: rows behind any slice without manually typing a filter.
 5537: 
 5538: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
 5539: 
 5540: These are the most common mistakes when working with this controller.
 5541: 
 5542: =over 4
 5543: 
 5544: =item B<SQLite files must use the .sql extension, not .sqlite>
 5545: 
 5546: C<Database::Abstraction> (the data-reading library) probes for a file called
 5547: C<tablename.sql> when it wants to open a SQLite database.  It does B<not>
 5548: look for C<.sqlite> or C<.db3>.  If your file is called C<inventory.sqlite>,
 5549: rename it to C<inventory.sql> or it will not appear in the file browser and
 5550: will return 404 when opened.
 5551: 
 5552: =item B<Filter values that contain a colon still work>
 5553: 
 5554: A date like C<2025-01-15> contains hyphens, not colons, so it is fine.  But
 5555: if your value itself contains a colon (for example, a time like C<14:30:00>),
 5556: the filter still works because the C<col:op:val> spec is split on the B<first
 5557: two> colons only -- the rest of the string becomes the value.
 5558: 
 5559:   # This correctly matches "14:30:00" in the start_time column:
 5560:   ?f=start_time:eq:14:30:00
 5561: 
 5562: =item B<Left join keeps only the FIRST matching right-table row>
 5563: 
 5564: When the right table has two rows with the same join key, only the first one
 5565: (in file order) is used.  The second is silently ignored.  If you need all
 5566: matches, consider pre-processing your data so join keys are unique.
 5567: 
 5568: =item B<Export format comes from the filename extension, not a Content-Type header>
 5569: 
 5570: When using C<POST /export> to save a file to disk, the format (CSV or SQLite)
 5571: is determined by the extension of the C<filename> parameter.  C<.csv> produces
 5572: a CSV file; C<.sql> produces a SQLite database.  Any other extension returns
 5573: HTTP 415 (Unsupported Media Type).  The C<Content-Type> request header is
 5574: ignored entirely.
 5575: 
 5576: =item B<Template Toolkit variables starting with underscore are silently dropped>
 5577: 
 5578: If you add a stash variable with a name starting with C<_> (for example,
 5579: C<_tmp> or C<_result>), Template Toolkit will silently produce an empty string
 5580: when the template tries to read it.  This is a TT quirk when C<TRIM =E<gt> 1>
 5581: is active.  Always use names that start with a letter.
 5582: 
 5583: =item B<_apply_filter_spec always passes all records through for unknown operators>
 5584: 
 5585: If you pass an operator that is not in the supported list (for example C<regex>
 5586: or C<like>), the filter is treated as a no-op and B<all rows are returned>.  No
 5587: error is produced.  This is intentional so that future operators can be added
 5588: without breaking existing clients that read a wider response.
 5589: 
 5590: =item B<Uploaded files are evicted automatically, but not immediately>
 5591: 
 5592: Files uploaded via C<POST /upload> are stored in C<.uploads/> under the
 5593: application home directory.  C<Database::BI> evicts upload subdirectories
 5594: whose modification time is older than 24 hours B<on every server startup>.
 5595: Between restarts, files accumulate.  For an immediate full purge (regardless
 5596: of age), post to C<POST /uploads/clear> or click the "Clear upload cache"
 5597: button in the UI.
 5598: 
 5599: =item B<Open C<data/> tables by name; open other files by absolute path>
 5600: 
 5601: The C<view> action (C<GET /view/:table>) only looks inside C<data_dir>.
 5602: To open a file from anywhere else on the filesystem, use C<open_file>
 5603: (C<GET /open?path=/abs/path>).  The two routes use different URL schemes and
 5604: are not interchangeable.
 5605: 
 5606: =back
 5607: 
 5608: =head1 LIMITATIONS
 5609: 
 5610: =over 4
 5611: 
 5612: =item *
 5613: 
 5614: Multi-table joins are delegated to C<Database::Join> with C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>.
 5615: For datasets up to C<join_max_rows> combined rows (default 10,000; configurable
 5616: in C<database_bi.conf>) the join runs in Perl memory; larger datasets spill to
 5617: a temporary SQLite database so peak RAM is bounded by the result set rather than
 5618: the sum of all source tables.
 5619: 
 5620: =item *
 5621: 
 5622: C<_resolve_language> extracts only the primary language subtag from the first
 5623: C<Accept-Language> tag (e.g. C<de> from C<de-DE,de;q=0.9,en;q=0.8>).
 5624: Quality weights and multiple alternatives are not ranked.
 5625: 
 5626: =item *
 5627: 
 5628: No GeoIP-based language resolution is implemented.  The language is resolved
 5629: from the HTTP C<Accept-Language> header only.
 5630: 
 5631: =item *
 5632: 
 5633: C<Sub::Protected> enforcement requires the CHECK compilation phase; when modules
 5634: are loaded dynamically at test time the "Too late to run CHECK block" warning
 5635: is emitted and the restriction is not enforced in that context.
 5636: 
 5637: =back
